# Computer Science Chapter Projects

One required applied project follows every chapter in the [Computer Science roadmap](../roadmaps/CS.md). Projects share a storyline inside each unit, but every chapter produces its own reviewable deliverable and commit.

<a id="cs-phase-1-project"></a>
### COMPUTER SCIENCE CHAPTER 1 PROJECT

#### PROJECT: THE SMART VENDING MACHINE CONTROLLER

#### ARTIFACT TYPE

analysis

#### CHAPTER OUTCOME

Design the conceptual system and complete algorithmic logic for a vending machine that sells items, accepts cash/coins, and dispenses change.

**Chapter topic:** What Is Computer Science?

**Format:** Hand-Written / On Paper (No Coding Needed)

**Why this format:** Unit I establishes computational thinking, decomposition, abstraction, and algorithms before touching code syntax.

#### LESSONS PRACTICED

- **1.1 — Problems, Inputs & Outputs:** Apply Problems, Inputs & Outputs to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.2 — Algorithms:** Apply Algorithms to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.3 — CS vs Programming vs Software Engineering:** Apply CS vs Programming vs Software Engineering to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.4 — Abstraction:** Apply Abstraction to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.5 — Decomposition:** Apply Decomposition to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.6 — Computational Thinking:** Apply Computational Thinking to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.
- **1.7 — PRACTICE — Written / By Hand:** Apply PRACTICE — Written / By Hand to The Smart Vending Machine Controller and record the reasoning and result.

#### SPECIFICATION

- **Format:** Hand-Written / On Paper (No Coding Needed)
- **Why this format:** Unit I establishes computational thinking, decomposition, abstraction, and algorithms before touching code syntax.
- **Problem statement:** Design the conceptual system and complete algorithmic logic for a vending machine that sells items, accepts cash/coins, and dispenses change.
- Build and complete the The Smart Vending Machine Controller according to the chapter requirements.
- Keep the scope focused, reproducible, and verifiable.

#### DELIVERABLES

- *I/O & Abstraction:** Identify 3 inputs, 2 outputs, 2 hidden implementation details, and 2 interface details.
- *Decomposition:** Draw a decomposition tree breaking the system into 3 main sub-components.
- *Precise Algorithm:** Write a numbered, unambiguous algorithm handling the normal flow, out-of-stock, and insufficient funds.
- *Hand-Trace:** Walk through purchasing a $2.50 item with a $5.00 bill.

#### TECH STACK

- Markdown notes or Pen & Paper / Notepad.
- Git for saving project progress.
- Diagramming / calculations where required.

#### BUILD IT STEP BY STEP

1. Review the chapter problem statement and format requirements.
2. Click **Start Building** to open the workspace with the preloaded solution.
3. Implement or step through each checklist item systematically.
4. Verify all edge cases and record the validation outcome.
5. Save your progress with a clean Git commit checkpoint.

#### VALIDATION

- **Normal case:** Complete the intended scenario and verify correct behavior or calculations against the solution.
- **Boundary case:** Test edge conditions, limits, or boundary inputs and verify robustness.
- **Safe failure case:** Verify error handling, exception safety, or invalid state recovery.
- **Reviewer check:** The completed work can be reviewed against the master answer key.

#### COMPLETION CRITERIA

- All checklist requirements are satisfied, validation passes, and the checkpoint commit is complete.


<a id="cs-phase-2-project"></a>
### COMPUTER SCIENCE CHAPTER 2 PROJECT

#### PROJECT: MANUAL 8-BIT DATA REPRESENTATION ENGINE

#### ARTIFACT TYPE

analysis

#### CHAPTER OUTCOME

Manually encode, convert, and detect overflow across multiple data types using 8-bit registers.

**Chapter topic:** How Computers Represent Information

**Format:** Hand-Written / Pen & Paper (Math & Bit Calculations)

**Why this format:** You must master binary, hex, two's complement, and overflow mechanics manually before letting a compiler hide them.

#### LESSONS PRACTICED

- **2.1 — Bits & Bytes:** Apply Bits & Bytes to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.2 — Binary:** Apply Binary to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.3 — Hexadecimal:** Apply Hexadecimal to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.4 — Signed / Unsigned Numbers:** Apply Signed / Unsigned Numbers to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.5 — Floating Point:** Apply Floating Point to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.6 — ASCII / Unicode / UTF-8:** Apply ASCII / Unicode / UTF-8 to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.7 — Integer Overflow:** Apply Integer Overflow to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.8 — Endianness:** Apply Endianness to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.
- **2.9 — PRACTICE — Mixed:** Apply PRACTICE — Mixed to Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine and record the reasoning and result.

#### SPECIFICATION

- **Format:** Hand-Written / Pen & Paper (Math & Bit Calculations)
- **Why this format:** You must master binary, hex, two's complement, and overflow mechanics manually before letting a compiler hide them.
- **Problem statement:** Manually encode, convert, and detect overflow across multiple data types using 8-bit registers.
- Build and complete the Manual 8-bit Data Representation Engine according to the chapter requirements.
- Keep the scope focused, reproducible, and verifiable.

#### DELIVERABLES

- *Base Conversion:** Convert decimal `157` and `-43` to 8-bit binary and hexadecimal.
- *Two's Complement Arithmetic:** Compute `45 + (-60)` in 8-bit two's complement and verify sign bits.
- *Overflow Analysis:** Calculate `120 + 15` in an 8-bit signed integer. State why signed overflow occurs.
- *Text Encoding:** Manually map the ASCII characters `"CS!"` to binary and hex byte sequences.

#### TECH STACK

- Markdown notes or Pen & Paper / Notepad.
- Git for saving project progress.
- Diagramming / calculations where required.

#### BUILD IT STEP BY STEP

1. Review the chapter problem statement and format requirements.
2. Click **Start Building** to open the workspace with the preloaded solution.
3. Implement or step through each checklist item systematically.
4. Verify all edge cases and record the validation outcome.
5. Save your progress with a clean Git commit checkpoint.

#### VALIDATION

- **Normal case:** Complete the intended scenario and verify correct behavior or calculations against the solution.
- **Boundary case:** Test edge conditions, limits, or boundary inputs and verify robustness.
- **Safe failure case:** Verify error handling, exception safety, or invalid state recovery.

<a id="cs-phase-3-project"></a>
### COMPUTER SCIENCE CHAPTER 3 PROJECT

#### PROJECT: 1-BIT FULL ADDER & 2-BIT ALU SCHEMATIC

#### ARTIFACT TYPE

analysis

#### CHAPTER OUTCOME

Construct truth tables and gate diagrams for a digital arithmetic circuit.

**Chapter topic:** Logic & Digital Computation

**Format:** Hand-Written / Circuit Diagram & Truth Tables

**Why this format:** Digital logic is rooted in Boolean algebra and hardware schematics before reaching software.

#### LESSONS PRACTICED

- **3.1 — Boolean Logic:** Apply Boolean Logic to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.2 — Truth Tables:** Apply Truth Tables to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.3 — AND / OR / NOT / XOR:** Apply AND / OR / NOT / XOR to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.4 — Logic Gates:** Apply Logic Gates to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.5 — Combinational Circuits:** Apply Combinational Circuits to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.6 — Sequential Circuits:** Apply Sequential Circuits to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.7 — Flip-Flops & State:** Apply Flip-Flops & State to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.
- **3.8 — PRACTICE — Diagram / By Hand:** Apply PRACTICE — Diagram / By Hand to 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic and record the reasoning and result.

#### SPECIFICATION

- **Format:** Hand-Written / Circuit Diagram & Truth Tables
- **Why this format:** Digital logic is rooted in Boolean algebra and hardware schematics before reaching software.
- **Problem statement:** Construct truth tables and gate diagrams for a digital arithmetic circuit.
- Build and complete the 1-Bit Full Adder & 2-Bit ALU Schematic according to the chapter requirements.
- Keep the scope focused, reproducible, and verifiable.

#### DELIVERABLES

- *Truth Tables:** Derive the full truth tables for AND, OR, XOR, NAND, and NOT.
- *Full Adder Design:** Combine two half adders and an OR gate to design a 1-bit full adder with inputs $A, B, C_{in}$ and outputs $Sum, C_{out}$.
- *2-Bit Ripple Adder:** Chain two full adders together and trace `11 + 01 = 100`.
- *ALU Control Logic:** Design a 1-bit multiplexer circuit that selects between AND and ADD based on an `Opcode` bit.

#### TECH STACK

- Markdown notes or Pen & Paper / Notepad.
- Git for saving project progress.
- Diagramming / calculations where required.

<a id="cs-phase-4-project"></a>
### COMPUTER SCIENCE CHAPTER 4 PROJECT

#### PROJECT: FETCH-DECODE-EXECUTE CPU TRACE

#### ARTIFACT TYPE

lab

#### CHAPTER OUTCOME

Hand-trace a 3-instruction assembly sequence through CPU registers, buses, and cache layers.

**Chapter topic:** Computer Hardware

**Format:** Hand-Written / Architectural Flowchart & Instruction Trace

**Why this format:** Understanding the Von Neumann architecture requires mentally tracing register transfers (PC, MAR, MDR, ALU).

#### LESSONS PRACTICED

- **4.1 — CPU:** Apply CPU to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.2 — Instructions:** Apply Instructions to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.3 — Registers:** Apply Registers to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.4 — Fetch → Decode → Execute:** Apply Fetch → Decode → Execute to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.5 — RAM:** Apply RAM to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.6 — Cache:** Apply Cache to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.7 — Storage:** Apply Storage to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.8 — Input / Output Devices:** Apply Input / Output Devices to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.9 — Buses:** Apply Buses to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.
- **4.10 — PRACTICE — Trace / Diagram:** Apply PRACTICE — Trace / Diagram to Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace and record the reasoning and result.

#### SPECIFICATION

- **Format:** Hand-Written / Architectural Flowchart & Instruction Trace
- **Why this format:** Understanding the Von Neumann architecture requires mentally tracing register transfers (PC, MAR, MDR, ALU).
- **Problem statement:** Hand-trace a 3-instruction assembly sequence through CPU registers, buses, and cache layers.
- Build and complete the Fetch-Decode-Execute CPU Trace according to the chapter requirements.
- Keep the scope focused, reproducible, and verifiable.

#### DELIVERABLES

- *Block Diagram:** Draw the CPU (ALU, Control Unit, Registers), System Bus, and RAM with data/address flow arrows.
- *Cycle-by-Cycle Trace:** Trace `LOAD R1, [100]`, `ADD R1, R2`, `STORE R1, [101]` step by step across PC, MAR, MDR, and IR.
- *Memory Hierarchy Ranking:** Rank Register, L1/L2/L3 Cache, RAM, and SSD by speed, cost, and typical capacity.
- *Cache Miss Scenario:** Explain in 2 sentences what occurs in hardware when an instruction causes a cache miss.

#### TECH STACK

- Markdown notes or Pen & Paper / Notepad.
- Git for saving project progress.
- Diagramming / calculations where required.

#### BUILD IT STEP BY STEP

2. Click **Start Building** to open the workspace with the preloaded solution.
3. Implement or step through each checklist item systematically.

<a id="cs-phase-5-project"></a>
### COMPUTER SCIENCE CHAPTER 5 PROJECT

#### PROJECT: COMPILATION PIPELINE & MEMORY LAYOUT MAP

#### ARTIFACT TYPE

analysis

#### CHAPTER OUTCOME

Map the complete journey of code from text to execution and diagram the OS process memory layout.

**Chapter topic:** From Source Code to a Running Program

**Format:** Hand-Written / Mental Model & Memory Layout Diagram

**Why this format:** Consolidates the boundary between source code, bytecode/machine code, compiler/interpreter, and process memory before writing programs.

#### LESSONS PRACTICED

- **5.1 — Source Code:** Apply Source Code to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.2 — Syntax & Semantics:** Apply Syntax & Semantics to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.3 — Compiler:** Apply Compiler to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.4 — Interpreter:** Apply Interpreter to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.5 — Bytecode:** Apply Bytecode to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.6 — JIT:** Apply JIT to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.7 — Lexer:** Apply Lexer to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.8 — Parser:** Apply Parser to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.9 — Linking:** Apply Linking to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.10 — Loading:** Apply Loading to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.
- **5.11 — MINI PROJECT — Mini Expression Runtime:** Apply MINI PROJECT — Mini Expression Runtime to Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map and record the reasoning and result.

#### SPECIFICATION

- **Format:** Hand-Written / Mental Model & Memory Layout Diagram
- **Why this format:** Consolidates the boundary between source code, bytecode/machine code, compiler/interpreter, and process memory before writing programs.
- **Problem statement:** Map the complete journey of code from text to execution and diagram the OS process memory layout.
- Build and complete the Compilation Pipeline & Memory Layout Map according to the chapter requirements.
- Keep the scope focused, reproducible, and verifiable.

#### DELIVERABLES

- *Pipeline Stages:** Draw the 4 stages from high-level source code to binary execution (Lexing/Parsing -> AST -> Bytecode/Machine Code -> Execution).
- *Compiler vs Interpreter:** Compare Ahead-Of-Time compilation vs Interpretation vs JIT compilation in a 3-column table.
- *Process Memory Diagram:** Draw a process memory layout showing Text (Code), Data, Heap (growing up), and Stack (growing down).
- *Stack Frame Trace:** Diagram the call stack frames for a function `foo()` calling `bar(x)`.

#### TECH STACK

- Markdown notes or Pen & Paper / Notepad.
- Git for saving project progress.
- Diagramming / calculations where required.

- Implement exponential backoff with full jitter for retries.
- Add request timeouts to prevent thread pool exhaustion.
